Um... I don't think so? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, really. 

I guess... well, in my line of work, you kind of have to make split-second decisions, right? You don't always have time to sit and ponder every angle when an alarm goes off. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... um, I mean, I try really hard to be respectful when people are talking, especially the brass. But... yeah, I guess I do get a little antsy sometimes. 

It's just, you know, we have these long command meetings, and... sitting still through them, it honestly feels like my body is just revving too high. Like my engine is idling too fast, if that makes sense? I just get this overwhelming urge to stand up, go walk the bay, check the rigs... just do literally anything besides stay planted in that chair. 

I really try to hide it, though! I don't want the chiefs thinking I'm not paying attention or that I don't care about what they're saying, because I do. It's just hard to keep my body quiet when we aren't actively doing something. Is that normal for guys in my line of work? I mean, we're kind of built to be moving, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful.

Or I just completely skipped some little check box that actually matters a lot for the safety review. \n\nIt's really embarrassing, honestly. I don't do it on purpose, I just... I don't know how I miss them. It's mostly just little paperwork things like that, though. I mean, everybody makes little typos when they're rushing sometimes, right? Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, really. \n\nI guess... well, in my line of work, you kind of have to make split-second decisions, right? You don't always have time to sit and ponder every angle when an alarm goes off. So I'm used to just jumping into action. But I wouldn't call that acting without thinking, it's more like... being decisive? Being ready. \n\nI don't know, I try to be really thoughtful about the big things in life. I don't just go off and do crazy stuff on a whim or anything. Is that what you mean? Like making big, reckless choices? Because I really try not to do that. I hope that's the right kind of answer... I just don't really see myself as an impulsive guy.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not actively engaged in a task?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I mean, I try really hard to be respectful when people are talking, especially the brass. But... yeah, I guess I do get a little antsy sometimes. \n\nIt's just, you know, we have these long command meetings, and... sitting still through them, it honestly feels like my body is just revving too high. Like my engine is idling too fast, if that makes sense? I just get this overwhelming urge to stand up, go walk the bay, check the rigs... just do literally anything besides stay planted in that chair. \n\nI really try to hide it, though! I don't want the chiefs thinking I'm not paying attention or that I don't care about what they're saying, because I do. It's just hard to keep my body quiet when we aren't actively doing something. Is that normal for guys in my line of work? I mean, we're kind of built to be moving, right? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... um, it's not just at the firehouse, I guess. I mean, I really try to be present at home, I do. My family is everything to me. But... yeah, even on my days off, I have a hard time just... doing quiet stuff with them. 

Like, if we try to sit down and watch a movie or something, I... well, I end up talking a lot, or I just start moving around. I'll get up before I've really been sitting that long, maybe go check the tools in the garage or find some little project to fix. I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, I just... I want to be helpful, you know? Keeping things maintained around the house. 

I hope my wife doesn't think I'm avoiding her or the kids. I'm really not! It's just hard for me to just sit there and do nothing when there's always something that could be worked on. I mean, it's good to be productive on your days off, right? Does that make sense, or... is that a bad thing?

I don't just go off and do crazy stuff on a whim or anything. Is that what you mean? Like making big, reckless choices? Because I really try not to do that. I hope that's the right kind of answer... I just don't really see myself as an impulsive guy.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not actively engaged in a task?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I mean, I try really hard to be respectful when people are talking, especially the brass. But... yeah, I guess I do get a little antsy sometimes. \n\nIt's just, you know, we have these long command meetings, and... sitting still through them, it honestly feels like my body is just revving too high. Like my engine is idling too fast, if that makes sense? I just get this overwhelming urge to stand up, go walk the bay, check the rigs... just do literally anything besides stay planted in that chair. \n\nI really try to hide it, though! I don't want the chiefs thinking I'm not paying attention or that I don't care about what they're saying, because I do. It's just hard to keep my body quiet when we aren't actively doing something. Is that normal for guys in my line of work? I mean, we're kind of built to be moving, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety at home or when you're not working, or is it mostly just at the firehouse or during meetings?\n\nPatient: Well... um, it's not just at the firehouse, I guess. I mean, I really try to be present at home, I do. My family is everything to me. But... yeah, even on my days off, I have a hard time just... doing quiet stuff with them. \n\nLike, if we try to sit down and watch a movie or something, I... well, I end up talking a lot, or I just start moving around. I'll get up before I've really been sitting that long, maybe go check the tools in the garage or find some little project to fix. I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, I just... I want to be helpful, you know? Keeping things maintained around the house. \n\nI hope my wife doesn't think I'm avoiding her or the kids. I'm really not! It's just hard for me to just sit there and do nothing when there's always something that could be worked on. I mean, it's good to be productive on your days off, right? Does that make sense, or... is that a bad thing?\n\n[Last Question]\nAre you experiencing any difficulties in your relationships with your family or friends outside of the firehouse?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 37 / firefighter / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, no, not really. I mean, my family is great, and my friends are mostly guys from the department, so we all kind of understand each other. 

I guess... well, sometimes my wife gets a little frustrated if I start a project around the house and don't quite finish it before starting another one, but... that's just normal married stuff, right? We don't really fight about it. I always mean to go back and finish things, and I think she knows that. I try really hard to be a good husband and dad. 

I don't think there's any major problems or anything. I mean, everyone has little bumps here and there, don't they? I hope I'm not painting a bad picture of my marriage, because we're really very happy. Is that... does that answer what you're asking?

Well... yeah, I guess that's kind of it. That's actually the main reason I came in to see you. I just... I feel like I'm always running hot, you know? Like I can't shut the motor off, no matter what day of the week it is or if we're supposed to be on vacation.

And, um... well, to be honest, lately, that whole 'never stopping' thing has been getting me into a little bit of trouble at the firehouse. Nothing major! Just... you know, I guess I can be a bit much when everyone else is trying to have downtime between calls. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and staying ready, but I guess it comes off wrong sometimes. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad firefighter or a difficult guy to work with. I really respect my crew. Is it... is it normal to feel like your engine is just stuck in drive like that?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, outside of work, I think I'm doing okay. Like I said, the projects around the house sometimes take a little longer, but I always get to them eventually. I really try to stay on top of things for my family. 

But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... it's really the work stuff that's the problem right now. I know you asked about outside of work, but... well, I was actually pulled into a performance and safety meeting recently. It wasn't a huge deal, I mean, they just wanted to talk about some things... but they mentioned that I've been having trouble tracking details consistently. 

I really pride myself on being a good firefighter, I swear I do. But I feel like this whole thing is starting to cost me the trust of the guys at the house. I was really hoping to move up into a leadership role soon, but now... I don't know, it feels like that might be slipping away because they don't feel like they can count on me with the little things. 

I'm trying so hard to fix it, I really am. I hope that doesn't make me sound careless. I take my job very seriously. Is it... is it helpful that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.

I don't just go off and do crazy stuff on a whim or anything. Is that what you mean? Like making big, reckless choices? Because I really try not to do that. I hope that's the right kind of answer... I just don't really see myself as an impulsive guy.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not actively engaged in a task?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I mean, I try really hard to be respectful when people are talking, especially the brass. But... yeah, I guess I do get a little antsy sometimes. \n\nIt's just, you know, we have these long command meetings, and... sitting still through them, it honestly feels like my body is just revving too high. Like my engine is idling too fast, if that makes sense? I just get this overwhelming urge to stand up, go walk the bay, check the rigs... just do literally anything besides stay planted in that chair. \n\nI really try to hide it, though! I don't want the chiefs thinking I'm not paying attention or that I don't care about what they're saying, because I do. It's just hard to keep my body quiet when we aren't actively doing something. Is that normal for guys in my line of work? I mean, we're kind of built to be moving, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety at home or when you're not working, or is it mostly just at the firehouse or during meetings?\n\nPatient: Well... um, it's not just at the firehouse, I guess. I mean, I really try to be present at home, I do. My family is everything to me. But... yeah, even on my days off, I have a hard time just... doing quiet stuff with them. \n\nLike, if we try to sit down and watch a movie or something, I... well, I end up talking a lot, or I just start moving around. I'll get up before I've really been sitting that long, maybe go check the tools in the garage or find some little project to fix. I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, I just... I want to be helpful, you know? Keeping things maintained around the house. \n\nI hope my wife doesn't think I'm avoiding her or the kids. I'm really not! It's just hard for me to just sit there and do nothing when there's always something that could be worked on. I mean, it's good to be productive on your days off, right? Does that make sense, or... is that a bad thing?\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your relationships with your family or friends outside of the firehouse?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, not really. I mean, my family is great, and my friends are mostly guys from the department, so we all kind of understand each other. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes my wife gets a little frustrated if I start a project around the house and don't quite finish it before starting another one, but... that's just normal married stuff, right? We don't really fight about it. I always mean to go back and finish things, and I think she knows that. I try really hard to be a good husband and dad. \n\nI don't think there's any major problems or anything. I mean, everyone has little bumps here and there, don't they? I hope I'm not painting a bad picture of my marriage, because we're really very happy. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and staying ready, but I guess it comes off wrong sometimes. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad firefighter or a difficult guy to work with. I really respect my crew. Like... well, for example, when we have command meetings and I have to sit still for a while... it just feels like my body is revving way too high. \n\nI try really hard to just sit there and pay attention, I promise I do, but... it's like I have this overwhelming urge to just stand up, walk the bay, check the rigs... honestly, just do anything besides stay planted in that chair. I usually manage to keep it together, though! I don't want to be disruptive or anything. \n\nI just... I feel like I always need to be doing something productive, you know? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm not taking the meetings seriously. I really do try to focus. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\n[Last Question]\nAre you finding it difficult to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like to do outside of work due to feeling restless or always 'on the go'?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 37 / firefighter / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\nmanifestation: \"Even on days off, I have a hard time doing quiet stuff with my family because I start talking, moving around, checking tools in the garage, or getting up before I’ve been sitting long.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really love spending time with my family on my days off, I promise I do. I don't want it to sound like I don't enjoy being with them, because they mean everything to me. 

But... yeah, I guess if I'm being completely honest, the quiet stuff is kind of hard for me. Like, if we're trying to just sit down and watch a movie or play a board game... I have a really hard time just staying there. I usually end up talking too much, or just moving around, or getting up before I've really been sitting very long at all. 

I just get this feeling like I need to be doing something useful, you know? So I'll wander out and start checking my tools in the garage, or just pacing around the house. I always try to come right back, though! I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, and I really hope my wife doesn't think I'm trying to avoid them. I just get so antsy when things are too quiet. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad husband or dad. I really do try my best to be present for them. Is that... is that what you were asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right examples.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, my routines are mostly okay, I think. I mean, I really try to stay on top of everything. But, um... I guess if I'm being totally honest, things do slip through the cracks sometimes. Not because I don't care! I really, honestly mean to get it all done. 

It's just... I don't know, I get moving too fast or I'm focused on the next thing, and I'll realize I completely forgot to pay a bill until the late fee hits, or... or I'll forget to call someone back. And sometimes at work, I'll forget to sign off on my training modules or bring the right paperwork for my gear when I come on shift. 

I always fix it as soon as I realize, though! I promise I'm not trying to be irresponsible. I just... my brain just kind of skips over things when I'm bouncing around. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm terrible at my job or anything. I really do try to keep it all straight. Does that... is that the kind of thing you mean?

But... there are times with the paperwork where I mess up. \n\nLike, I’ll finish up an incident report and hand it in thinking it’s completely solid, you know? Ready to go. And then... well, the captain will have to pull me aside and point out that I put down the wrong unit number, or I missed a time stamp somewhere in the middle. Or I just completely skipped some little check box that actually matters a lot for the safety review. \n\nIt's really embarrassing, honestly. I don't do it on purpose, I just... I don't know how I miss them. It's mostly just little paperwork things like that, though. I mean, everybody makes little typos when they're rushing sometimes, right? Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, really. \n\nI guess... well, in my line of work, you kind of have to make split-second decisions, right? You don't always have time to sit and ponder every angle when an alarm goes off. So I'm used to just jumping into action. But I wouldn't call that acting without thinking, it's more like... being decisive? Being ready. \n\nI don't know, I try to be really thoughtful about the big things in life. I don't just go off and do crazy stuff on a whim or anything. Is that what you mean? Like making big, reckless choices? Because I really try not to do that. I hope that's the right kind of answer... I just don't really see myself as an impulsive guy.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not actively engaged in a task?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I mean, I try really hard to be respectful when people are talking, especially the brass. But... yeah, I guess I do get a little antsy sometimes. \n\nIt's just, you know, we have these long command meetings, and... sitting still through them, it honestly feels like my body is just revving too high. Like my engine is idling too fast, if that makes sense? I just get this overwhelming urge to stand up, go walk the bay, check the rigs... just do literally anything besides stay planted in that chair. \n\nI really try to hide it, though! I don't want the chiefs thinking I'm not paying attention or that I don't care about what they're saying, because I do. It's just hard to keep my body quiet when we aren't actively doing something. Is that normal for guys in my line of work? I mean, we're kind of built to be moving, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety at home or when you're not working, or is it mostly just at the firehouse or during meetings?\n\nPatient: Well... um, it's not just at the firehouse, I guess. I mean, I really try to be present at home, I do. My family is everything to me. But... yeah, even on my days off, I have a hard time just... doing quiet stuff with them. \n\nLike, if we try to sit down and watch a movie or something, I... well, I end up talking a lot, or I just start moving around. I'll get up before I've really been sitting that long, maybe go check the tools in the garage or find some little project to fix. I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, I just... I want to be helpful, you know? Keeping things maintained around the house. \n\nI hope my wife doesn't think I'm avoiding her or the kids. I'm really not! It's just hard for me to just sit there and do nothing when there's always something that could be worked on. I mean, it's good to be productive on your days off, right? Does that make sense, or... is that a bad thing?\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your relationships with your family or friends outside of the firehouse?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, not really. I mean, my family is great, and my friends are mostly guys from the department, so we all kind of understand each other. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes my wife gets a little frustrated if I start a project around the house and don't quite finish it before starting another one, but... that's just normal married stuff, right? We don't really fight about it. I always mean to go back and finish things, and I think she knows that. I try really hard to be a good husband and dad. \n\nI don't think there's any major problems or anything. I mean, everyone has little bumps here and there, don't they? I hope I'm not painting a bad picture of my marriage, because we're really very happy. Is that... does that answer what you're asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're always 'on the go' and can't relax, even on weekends or during vacations?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess that's kind of it. That's actually the main reason I came in to see you. I just... I feel like I'm always running hot, you know? Like I can't shut the motor off, no matter what day of the week it is or if we're supposed to be on vacation. \n\nI really try to relax when I'm supposed to, I promise I do! But... it's just hard. And, um... well, to be honest, lately, that whole 'never stopping' thing has been getting me into a little bit of trouble at the firehouse. Nothing major! Just... you know, I guess I can be a bit much when everyone else is trying to have downtime between calls. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and staying ready, but I guess it comes off wrong sometimes. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad firefighter or a difficult guy to work with. I really respect my crew. Is it... is it normal to feel like your engine is just stuck in drive like that?\n\nDoctor: Are your family and friends outside of the firehouse noticing any changes in your behavior or mood lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, my wife hasn't really said anything about me acting different or being in a bad mood or anything like that. I try really hard to leave any stress at the door when I get home. \n\nLike I said, my friends are mostly guys from work, so they see me all the time anyway. We don't really talk about feelings or moods or anything... we just kind of hang out. So, no, I haven't noticed anyone outside of work saying anything. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I'm not trying to hide anything, I just... I don't think there's been any changes like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your ability to complete tasks or achieve your goals outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um... well, outside of work, I think I'm doing okay. Like I said, the projects around the house sometimes take a little longer, but I always get to them eventually. I really try to stay on top of things for my family. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... it's really the work stuff that's the problem right now. I know you asked about outside of work, but... well, I was actually pulled into a performance and safety meeting recently. It wasn't a huge deal, I mean, they just wanted to talk about some things... but they mentioned that I've been having trouble tracking details consistently. \n\nI really pride myself on being a good firefighter, I swear I do. But I feel like this whole thing is starting to cost me the trust of the guys at the house. I was really hoping to move up into a leadership role soon, but now... I don't know, it feels like that might be slipping away because they don't feel like they can count on me with the little things. \n\nI'm trying so hard to fix it, I really am. I hope that doesn't make me sound careless. I take my job very seriously. Is it... is it helpful that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and being 'on the go' at the same level when you're alone compared to when you're with others?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think it's pretty much the same whether I'm by myself or with the crew. It's just always there, you know? Like... well, for example, when we have command meetings and I have to sit still for a while... it just feels like my body is revving way too high. \n\nI try really hard to just sit there and pay attention, I promise I do, but... it's like I have this overwhelming urge to just stand up, walk the bay, check the rigs... honestly, just do anything besides stay planted in that chair. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm not taking the meetings seriously. I really do try to focus. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like to do outside of work due to feeling restless or always 'on the go'?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really love spending time with my family on my days off, I promise I do. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, it hasn't always been this bad! I mean, I've always had a lot of energy, which is usually a good thing in my line of work, right? But... as far as it being a real problem where I just can't settle down... 

I'd say it's been about a year and five months? Give or take? That's when we started doing these longer rotating shifts, and they added all this extra documentation we have to do. Oh, and I got assigned drone duties, too! Which is really cool, I was so excited they trusted me with it. But it's just... it's a lot more on my plate all at once. 

Since then, it just feels like my motor is always running, even when I'm off shift. I try really hard to wind down, I swear I do. I don't want to be bouncing off the walls at home or making my crew crazy. I just... it's been harder lately to find that off switch since the schedule changed. Is that... does that timeline make sense for what you're asking? I hope I'm explaining it okay.

I try really hard to leave any stress at the door when I get home. \n\nLike I said, my friends are mostly guys from work, so they see me all the time anyway. We don't really talk about feelings or moods or anything... we just kind of hang out. So, no, I haven't noticed anyone outside of work saying anything. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I'm not trying to hide anything, I just... I don't think there's been any changes like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your ability to complete tasks or achieve your goals outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um... well, outside of work, I think I'm doing okay. I guess, if I'm being completely honest... it's really the work stuff that's the problem right now. I know you asked about outside of work, but... well, I was actually pulled into a performance and safety meeting recently. It wasn't a huge deal, I mean, they just wanted to talk about some things... but they mentioned that I've been having trouble tracking details consistently. \n\nI really pride myself on being a good firefighter, I swear I do. But I feel like this whole thing is starting to cost me the trust of the guys at the house. I was really hoping to move up into a leadership role soon, but now... I don't know, it feels like that might be slipping away because they don't feel like they can count on me with the little things. \n\nI'm trying so hard to fix it, I really am. I hope that doesn't make me sound careless. I take my job very seriously. Is it... is it helpful that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and being 'on the go' at the same level when you're alone compared to when you're with others?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think it's pretty much the same whether I'm by myself or with the crew. It's just always there, you know? Like... well, for example, when we have command meetings and I have to sit still for a while... it just feels like my body is revving way too high. \n\nI try really hard to just sit there and pay attention, I promise I do, but... it's like I have this overwhelming urge to just stand up, walk the bay, check the rigs... honestly, just do anything besides stay planted in that chair. I usually manage to keep it together, though! I don't want to be disruptive or anything. \n\nI just... I feel like I always need to be doing something productive, you know? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm not taking the meetings seriously. I really do try to focus. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Are these troubles at the firehouse causing you significant stress or problems with your relationships with colleagues?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I mean, I love my crew, we're a brotherhood, right? So I don't want to make it sound like we're fighting or anything. But... yeah, I guess it is causing some stress. I'm a little embarrassed to even admit this, honestly, but... they actually pulled me into a, uh, a performance and safety meeting recently. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad firefighter, I really do try my best every single shift. \n\nIt's just... I think it's starting to cost me some trust with the guys. I've been working really hard for a shot at moving into leadership, and now it feels like that's kind of slipping away. The crew just feels like they can't always count on me to track all the details consistently right now. Am I explaining that okay? I just really want to fix it so I don't let them down.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I hate to admit it, but... yeah, I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I always try to do my absolute best, I really do. But... there are times with the paperwork where I mess up. \n\nLike, I’ll finish up an incident report and hand it in thinking it’s completely solid, you know? Ready to go. And then... well, the captain will have to pull me aside and point out that I put down the wrong unit number, or I missed a time stamp somewhere in the middle. Or I just completely skipped some little check box that actually matters a lot for the safety review. \n\nIt's really embarrassing, honestly. I don't do it on purpose, I just... I don't know how I miss them. It's mostly just little paperwork things like that, though. I mean, everybody makes little typos when they're rushing sometimes, right? Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, really. \n\nI guess... well, in my line of work, you kind of have to make split-second decisions, right? You don't always have time to sit and ponder every angle when an alarm goes off. So I'm used to just jumping into action. But I wouldn't call that acting without thinking, it's more like... being decisive? Being ready. \n\nI don't know, I try to be really thoughtful about the big things in life. I don't just go off and do crazy stuff on a whim or anything. Is that what you mean? Like making big, reckless choices? Because I really try not to do that. I hope that's the right kind of answer... I just don't really see myself as an impulsive guy.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not actively engaged in a task?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I mean, I try really hard to be respectful when people are talking, especially the brass. But... yeah, I guess I do get a little antsy sometimes. \n\nIt's just, you know, we have these long command meetings, and... sitting still through them, it honestly feels like my body is just revving too high. Like my engine is idling too fast, if that makes sense? I just get this overwhelming urge to stand up, go walk the bay, check the rigs... just do literally anything besides stay planted in that chair. \n\nI really try to hide it, though! I don't want the chiefs thinking I'm not paying attention or that I don't care about what they're saying, because I do. It's just hard to keep my body quiet when we aren't actively doing something. Is that normal for guys in my line of work? I mean, we're kind of built to be moving, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety at home or when you're not working, or is it mostly just at the firehouse or during meetings?\n\nPatient: Well... um, it's not just at the firehouse, I guess. I mean, I really try to be present at home, I do. My family is everything to me. But... yeah, even on my days off, I have a hard time just... doing quiet stuff with them. \n\nLike, if we try to sit down and watch a movie or something, I... well, I end up talking a lot, or I just start moving around. I'll get up before I've really been sitting that long, maybe go check the tools in the garage or find some little project to fix. I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, I just... I want to be helpful, you know? Keeping things maintained around the house. \n\nI hope my wife doesn't think I'm avoiding her or the kids. I'm really not! It's just hard for me to just sit there and do nothing when there's always something that could be worked on. I mean, it's good to be productive on your days off, right? Does that make sense, or... is that a bad thing?\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your relationships with your family or friends outside of the firehouse?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, not really. I mean, my family is great, and my friends are mostly guys from the department, so we all kind of understand each other. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes my wife gets a little frustrated if I start a project around the house and don't quite finish it before starting another one, but... that's just normal married stuff, right? We don't really fight about it. I always mean to go back and finish things, and I think she knows that. I try really hard to be a good husband and dad. \n\nI don't think there's any major problems or anything. I mean, everyone has little bumps here and there, don't they? I hope I'm not painting a bad picture of my marriage, because we're really very happy. Is that... does that answer what you're asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're always 'on the go' and can't relax, even on weekends or during vacations?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess that's kind of it. That's actually the main reason I came in to see you. I just... I feel like I'm always running hot, you know? Like I can't shut the motor off, no matter what day of the week it is or if we're supposed to be on vacation. \n\nI really try to relax when I'm supposed to, I promise I do! But... it's just hard. And, um... well, to be honest, lately, that whole 'never stopping' thing has been getting me into a little bit of trouble at the firehouse. Nothing major! Just... you know, I guess I can be a bit much when everyone else is trying to have downtime between calls. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and staying ready, but I guess it comes off wrong sometimes. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad firefighter or a difficult guy to work with. I really respect my crew. Is it... is it normal to feel like your engine is just stuck in drive like that?\n\nDoctor: Are your family and friends outside of the firehouse noticing any changes in your behavior or mood lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, my wife hasn't really said anything about me acting different or being in a bad mood or anything like that. I try really hard to leave any stress at the door when I get home. \n\nLike I said, my friends are mostly guys from work, so they see me all the time anyway. We don't really talk about feelings or moods or anything... we just kind of hang out. So, no, I haven't noticed anyone outside of work saying anything. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I'm not trying to hide anything, I just... I don't think there's been any changes like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your ability to complete tasks or achieve your goals outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um... well, outside of work, I think I'm doing okay. Like I said, the projects around the house sometimes take a little longer, but I always get to them eventually. I really try to stay on top of things for my family. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... it's really the work stuff that's the problem right now. I know you asked about outside of work, but... well, I was actually pulled into a performance and safety meeting recently. It wasn't a huge deal, I mean, they just wanted to talk about some things... but they mentioned that I've been having trouble tracking details consistently. \n\nI really pride myself on being a good firefighter, I swear I do. But I feel like this whole thing is starting to cost me the trust of the guys at the house. I was really hoping to move up into a leadership role soon, but now... I don't know, it feels like that might be slipping away because they don't feel like they can count on me with the little things. \n\nI'm trying so hard to fix it, I really am. I hope that doesn't make me sound careless. I take my job very seriously. Is it... is it helpful that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and being 'on the go' at the same level when you're alone compared to when you're with others?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think it's pretty much the same whether I'm by myself or with the crew. It's just always there, you know? Like... well, for example, when we have command meetings and I have to sit still for a while... it just feels like my body is revving way too high. \n\nI try really hard to just sit there and pay attention, I promise I do, but... it's like I have this overwhelming urge to just stand up, walk the bay, check the rigs... honestly, just do anything besides stay planted in that chair. I usually manage to keep it together, though! I don't want to be disruptive or anything. \n\nI just... I feel like I always need to be doing something productive, you know? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm not taking the meetings seriously. I really do try to focus. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like to do outside of work due to feeling restless or always 'on the go'?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really love spending time with my family on my days off, I promise I do. I don't want it to sound like I don't enjoy being with them, because they mean everything to me. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess if I'm being completely honest, the quiet stuff is kind of hard for me. Like, if we're trying to just sit down and watch a movie or play a board game... I have a really hard time just staying there. I usually end up talking too much, or just moving around, or getting up before I've really been sitting very long at all. \n\nI just get this feeling like I need to be doing something useful, you know? I just get so antsy when things are too quiet. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad husband or dad. I really do try my best to be present for them. Is that... is that what you were asking about? I always make sure to apologize if I'm being a distraction. And with the kids, I think they actually kind of like that I'm always ready to go do something active, you know? \n\nI really try to make sure my energy is put to good use so it doesn't bother anyone. I hope that makes sense? I just don't want you to think I'm causing trouble for my family because I can't sit still. I'm doing okay with them, I promise. Is that... does that answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it hard to manage your daily routines and responsibilities outside of work due to your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Um... well, my routines are mostly okay, I think. I really, honestly mean to get it all done. \n\nIt's just... I don't know, I get moving too fast or I'm focused on the next thing, and I'll realize I completely forgot to pay a bill until the late fee hits, or... or I'll forget to call someone back. I promise I'm not trying to be irresponsible. I just... my brain just kind of skips over things when I'm bouncing around. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm terrible at my job or anything. I really do try to keep it all straight. Does that... is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to fulfill your responsibilities or complete tasks outside of work due to your restlessness and inability to relax?\n\nPatient: Um... well, at home I really try my best to get all my chores done. I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, and I really hope my wife doesn't think I'm trying to avoid them. I just get so antsy when things are too quiet. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad husband or dad. I really do try my best to be present for them. Um... yeah, definitely more than six months. Like I mentioned a minute ago, I'd say it's been a real issue for about a year and five months?

But... yeah, I guess I do get a little antsy sometimes. \n\nIt's just, you know, we have these long command meetings, and... sitting still through them, it honestly feels like my body is just revving too high. Like my engine is idling too fast, if that makes sense? I just get this overwhelming urge to stand up, go walk the bay, check the rigs... just do literally anything besides stay planted in that chair. \n\nI really try to hide it, though! I don't want the chiefs thinking I'm not paying attention or that I don't care about what they're saying, because I do. It's just hard to keep my body quiet when we aren't actively doing something. Is that normal for guys in my line of work? I mean, we're kind of built to be moving, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety at home or when you're not working, or is it mostly just at the firehouse or during meetings?\n\nPatient: Well... um, it's not just at the firehouse, I guess. I mean, I really try to be present at home, I do. My family is everything to me. But... yeah, even on my days off, I have a hard time just... doing quiet stuff with them. \n\nLike, if we try to sit down and watch a movie or something, I... well, I end up talking a lot, or I just start moving around. I'll get up before I've really been sitting that long, maybe go check the tools in the garage or find some little project to fix. I don't mean to ruin their relaxing time, I just... I want to be helpful, you know? Keeping things maintained around the house. \n\nI hope my wife doesn't think I'm avoiding her or the kids. I'm really not! It's just hard for me to just sit there and do nothing when there's always something that could be worked on. I mean, it's good to be productive on your days off, right? Does that make sense, or... is that a bad thing?\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your relationships with your family or friends outside of the firehouse?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, not really. I mean, my family is great, and my friends are mostly guys from the department, so we all kind of understand each other. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes my wife gets a little frustrated if I start a project around the house and don't quite finish it before starting another one, but... that's just normal married stuff, right? We don't really fight about it. I always mean to go back and finish things, and I think she knows that. I try really hard to be a good husband and dad. \n\nI don't think there's any major problems or anything. I mean, everyone has little bumps here and there, don't they? I hope I'm not painting a bad picture of my marriage, because we're really very happy. Is that... does that answer what you're asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're always 'on the go' and can't relax, even on weekends or during vacations?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess that's kind of it. I want to be helpful, you know? Keeping things maintained around the house. \n\nI hope my wife doesn't think I'm avoiding her or the kids. I'm really not! It's just hard for me to just sit there and do nothing when there's always something that could be worked on. I mean, it's good to be productive on your days off, right? We don't really fight about it. I always mean to go back and finish things, and I think she knows that. I try really hard to be a good husband and dad. \n\nI don't think there's any major problems or anything. I mean, everyone has little bumps here and there, don't they? I hope I'm not painting a bad picture of my marriage, because we're really very happy.
